    66	
    67	## 3. Design
    68	
    69	### Part H, the harness (`scripts/uat/wispr_eyes.py`)
    70	H1. **The take's evidence is parsed, not eyeballed.** `_insertion_line(text)` (proposed) parses one `insertion api=` line into `{api, route, written, returned, evidence, outcome, attempts, ms, overrun, target}`; `last_take()` returns it as `insertion` (the stale `Insertion completed via` key goes) and counts `insertion api=` lines in its window (`insertions` (proposed)). `dictate_emulator` and `debug_insert` require `insertions == 1`, `outcome == "VERIFIED"`, `written == "true"`, `target == target_package`, else `ISSUE: …` naming the field; the route is its own line `VERIFIED: route=COMMIT` (or `PASTE`, still a pass; `route` is reported, never assumed). `dictate_emulator(..., route=None)` (proposed) may REQUIRE a route: `route="COMMIT"` turns a PASTE into `ISSUE`.
    71	H2. **The content verdict is exact, against the caller's literal.** `dictate_emulator(sentence, target_package, expected_final)` and `debug_insert(text, target_package, expected_final)` require the caller's literal (`expected_final` (proposed)) WHOLE final editor value; omission returns `BLOCKED: expected_final is required …` before acting. It is never derived from the spoken sentence or from `before + text`: the final value carries the speech engine's punctuation and `InsertionText`'s capitalisation and trailing-space rules (`InsertionText.kt:52-74,88-95`, proved by `InsertionTextTest.kt:33-38,68-75`); the deterministic cleanup neither capitalises the first character nor appends a period (`DeterministicCleanup.kt:516-520`, `DictationSessionCoordinator.kt:1188-1203`) (grounded round 1). The line is `VERIFIED: the editor's whole text now equals the expectation (N chars)` or `ISSUE: the editor's whole text differs from the expectation; it ends …`. Callers updated: `scripts/uat/scenes-115/force_stop_scene.py:57`, `wedge_scenes.py:97`, `scripts/uat/benchmark_front_door.py:348,356` pass the literal they expect. `_sentence_landed` (removed) is deleted (its only callers were these two); `_plain` stays for `find`.
    72	H3. **Only a uniquely focused editor is a target, and that choice is made BEFORE the take.** `_focused_field`: `chosen = [n for n in fields if n["focused"]]`; none → `None`; more than one → `Blocked("N editors are focused …")`. The AFTER read is `_field_by_identity(package, identity)` (proposed): it resolves exactly one editor by the saved `(package, resource id or bounds)` identity and reads its whole text whether or not focus has returned to it (the harness's own launcher pauses the editor and hides its keyboard, `PasteAccessibilityService.kt:434-443`; the recorder and stop launchers are 1×1 non-focusable and finish at once, `VoiceInputActivity.kt:79-97`; the stop path settles 1.5 s, `wispr_eyes.py:1432-1457`); current focus after the take is reported as a NOTE, never part of the landed verdict (grounded round 1). `focus_field` already reads back focus.
    73	H4. **`bound()` reads the whole block, and the exact component.** The `Bound services:{…}` entries carry the app LABEL only (`Service[label=EnviousWispr, feedbackType[…], …]`, captured 2026-09-22; no component in that block), and the component is in `Enabled services:{{com.envi.wispr/com.envi.wispr.paste.PasteAccessibilityService}}`. So: `re.search(r"Bound services:\{(.*)\}\s*$", out, re.M)` must contain `label=EnviousWispr` anywhere in the block, AND the `Enabled services` block must contain the exact `ACCESSIBILITY_SERVICE` component; either missing → `False`; a missing `Bound services:` line stays `Blocked` (coverage F7, modified: the block cannot name the component, so the label is matched where it lives and the component where it lives).
    74	H5. **Evidence is bounded to the owned take.** `clear_log()` records the device clock (`date '+%m-%d %H:%M:%S.000'`) in `_STATE["log_since"]` (proposed key); `last_take()` and `recording()` read `logcat -d -T '<since>'` when it is set and the WHOLE buffer with `"bounded": False` in the result when it is not; `_wait_for_the_take_to_finish` (today a `logcat -d` loop, `wispr_eyes.py:3225-3233`) opens ONE blocking `logcat -T '<since>'` stream and returns only on the current take's `insertion api=` line or its terminal failure line (`Take ended: …`), never looping over snapshots; its timeout is `UNKNOWN`/`BLOCKED`, never a product verdict (grounded round 4); `last_take()` requires exactly one `recording_start` in the window, else `{"unknown": "N takes in the window"}` and the callers report `ISSUE: … UNKNOWN`.
    75	H6. **Recording off is `NOT RUN`.** Every report-returning `_recording_is_off()` caller returns `NOT RUN: {RECORDING_IS_OFF}` before staging (three suites after H9, §10); the guard's own two raises inside `open_recorder` stay; `RECORDING_IS_OFF` text unchanged.
    76	H7. **The book is keyed by hardware identity.** Identity is cached PER TRANSPORT, never as one scalar: `_identity_of(transport)` (proposed) reads `ro.serialno` once per transport into `_STATE["identities"]` (proposed key), and `_book_key(transport)` (proposed) returns it; `_owe_locked`, `_owed`, `_settled_locked` use the key of the current transport. `restore()` (which today enumerates `_journal_read()` directly at `:3150` and compares raw keys with attached transport serials at `:3148-3158`, switching device with transport state only, `:2989-2994`) resolves EVERY attached transport to its identity under `_journal_locked`, migrates every attached transport-keyed debt under its identity key (a MOVE, never a drop), enumerates the identity-keyed debts, and uses the matching transport for the device operations that pay them; `_as_serial` carries both transport and identity. The literal `host` scope is never re-keyed. `restore()` reports which key it paid (coverage F4).
    77	H8. **An owned screen recording.** `screen_recording(path)` (proposed) context manager. Before starting: enumerate every `screenrecord` pid with its `/proc/<pid>/cmdline`; any existing `/system/bin/screenrecord` is `BLOCKED` naming every pid and command (never adopted, never killed). Then journal `("screenrecord", token)` BEFORE `adb shell screenrecord --time-limit 180 /sdcard/wispr-eyes/<token>.mp4` starts in the background; after starting, require exactly one pid whose full command line contains the owned output token (zero, an exit before a non-empty file, or more than one → fail closed, the debt kept). Stop with `kill -2` to that verified pid only, then require that pid to EXIT (the normal path waits on the local `adb shell screenrecord` process handle; bound 15 s) before the file is read once for size, pulled to `path`, deleted on the device, and the debt settled; file size is never completion. `restore()` pays a leftover by the same pid-and-cmdline identification, and while that pid or any token-bearing `screenrecord` command still exists it keeps the debt and refuses to delete or settle (grounded rounds 1 and 4; `tools-and-apps.md` RULE: identify-a-process-by-path-never-by-name). `dictate_emulator(..., record=None)`: a host path turns it on; the report carries `NOTE: screen recording at <path>` and the file's size; it is evidence to inspect, never a verdict.

Historical `session-log.md` entries naming them stay as history.

    81	### Part T, the device test (`app/src/androidTest`)
    82	T0. **The class-wide `@Before`/`@After` go** (`VoicePipelineDeviceTest.kt:90-103` binds ASR and polish, requires the cached fixture and warms S1 for every row; coverage F1). Only `transcribesThenPolishesWithSavedCustomWords` binds the services, requires the fixture and warms S1, inside itself; T1/T3/T4 require neither the fixture nor S1 when `audio=external`; `audio=speaker` alone checks the fixture with `assumeTrue`.
    83	T1. **`launcherRecordsPhonePlaybackAndReachesClipboardStep` (removed) is replaced** by `aSideButtonTakeLandsInTheFocusedEditorExactlyOnce` (proposed): stage `PasteTargetActivity` (field A focused, both receipt files initialised to `""` by its `onCreate`, T3); register the phase listener; start `VoiceInputActivity` (the side-button stand-in, `android-tooling.md` FACT: driving-the-app-from-the-command-line); await `LISTENING` (20 s bound, the failure names the phase seen); the audio arrives (T2); await the History row's insertion outcome through `observeAll()` (a new row with `insertionResult` in `{committed, pasted}` and `status` final; 90 s bound naming the last row seen); then assert: the receipt file's text `== EXPECTED_TEXT` (a literal, exactly once: `count(EXPECTED_TEXT) == 1`), `insertionResult == "committed"` on API 33+ with this field (the route), and `handoff` from the owner's line is not consulted for the verdict (the log stays diagnostic). No polish engine assertion (the emulator has none; the polish limb's outcome is `transcribesThenPolishesWithSavedCustomWords`'s business).
    84	T2. **The audio is fed by the driver.** The test takes an instrumentation argument `audio` (`-e audio external|speaker`, default `external`): `external` means the test publishes READY on the instrumentation stream once its own listener has seen the owner's LISTENING phase, and WAITS; `speaker` plays the fixture through the `SpeakerAudio` source (kept for the phone with no driver present), which installs an `AudioTrack.OnPlaybackPositionUpdateListener` (external), sets the notification marker to the PCM frame count and waits on `onMarkerReached` (external) with a bound of the clip length plus 5 s; the `Thread.sleep(clip length)` at `VoicePipelineDeviceTest.kt:351-360` goes; a marker never reached fails the row's staging precondition (grounded round 4). The harness gains `run_device_test(test_class, sentence, ...)` (proposed): calls `device()` first; installs nothing; runs `am instrument -w -r -e class <cls> -e audio external com.envi.wispr.test/androidx.test.runner.AndroidJUnitRunner` in the background and reads its `-r` stream. The TEST publishes readiness on that stream: after its in-process phase listener observes `LISTENING` and the row's staging is complete (T3 only after B has taken focus), it calls `Instrumentation.sendStatus` (external) with the bundle `driver_phase=READY` (proposed key) and the reserved result code `DRIVER_READY_STATUS` (proposed) (a value no runner uses); raw `am instrument -r` prints every bundle's keys followed by its `INSTRUMENTATION_STATUS_CODE`, so the driver's parser groups each `INSTRUMENTATION_STATUS` (external) bundle with its following code, treats a group carrying `driver_phase=READY` and that code as a driver-control event (never a test verdict), and every other group as a runner result (grounded round 2); on that event it injects at once (`inject_audio(pcm)` on the emulator; on the phone it refuses: the phone's driver is `run.py`, and `RECORDING_IS_OFF` is checked first); when `inject_audio` returns, the driver sends the explicit broadcast `ACTION_EXTERNAL_AUDIO_DONE` (proposed) to `com.envi.wispr` carrying the run's `driver_token` (proposed instrumentation argument, a random hex the driver minted); the test registered an exported, token-validating receiver for it BEFORE publishing READY, waits on it, then sends STOP (a mismatched or missing token is ignored; a 60 s timeout is `BLOCKED` staging) (grounded round 5). The driver returns the stream parsed for the runner's status-code lines (`INSTRUMENTATION_STATUS_CODE` (external)) and the failure text. No preference-file polling: `DictationSurfaceState.update` writes with `apply()` (`DictationSurfaceState.kt:23-27`), so the file lags the listener. No 3 s warm-up: `LISTENING` is published only after the capture process admitted and wrote its first audio block (`AudioCaptureService.kt:595-621`, `DictationSessionCoordinator.kt:763-824`), which IS the capture-ready signal; `SILENCE_STATUS_READY` is the optional auto-stop detector's, not a prerequisite (grounded round 1, findings 3 and 4). `dictate_emulator`'s own 3 s sleep before injection is replaced by `_wait_for_owner_listening()` (proposed), called after entering `open_recorder` and before `inject_audio`: it opens ONE blocking, bounded `logcat` reader (`logcat -T '<since>' -v time DictationSession:I '*:S'`, the window of H5) and returns on the first `Recording started (live after …)` line, never looping over `logcat -d`; its timeout (20 s) reports `BLOCKED: the owner never went live …`, an UNKNOWN about the harness's staging, never a product failure (grounded round 3). The line is the current take's owner line, written right after the LISTENING phase (`DictationSessionCoordinator.kt:763-824`, after the capture process admitted its first block, `AudioCaptureService.kt:595-621`). `recording_start` alone is too early (it is the capture process's own start line, `AudioCaptureService.kt:476-486`), and `open_recorder(verify=False)` today awaits nothing: it sends the start, sleeps 1.5 s + 1 s and yields (`wispr_eyes.py:1743-1763`) (grounded round 2).
    85	T3. **The two-editor side-button case (REF-01).** `PasteTargetActivity` gains an intent extra `two_fields` (proposed): a second `EditText` B below A with its own receipt `paste-target-b-received.txt`. On creation the activity atomically writes `""` to BOTH receipt files BEFORE installing the text watchers (today it deletes the file and creates it only on the first change, `PasteTargetActivity.kt:55-62`; coverage F5), so an untouched B is an EXISTING empty receipt and a disabled editor write leaves A's receipt empty, which fails. Row `aTakeStartedInAAndFocusMovedToBInsertsNowhereAndKeepsTheWordsOnTheClipboard` (proposed; AS BUILT, the plan's first draft named it `…LandsInAOnly` and expected the words in A, and the first live run said otherwise: the paste service waits for the ORIGINAL editor to return and gives up, `outcome=NEVER_RETURNED attempts=20`, row `insertion_interrupted`/`copy_only`, which is `architecture-rules.md` RULE: insertion-fails-safe-never-silently, so the row asserts the fail-safe): start with A focused, await LISTENING, move focus to B through the activity's own runtime-registered receiver (`ACTION_FOCUS_B` (proposed), registered with `Context.RECEIVER_EXPORTED` (external) on API 33+ because the sender is the app process and the activity the test package's UID, unregistered in `onDestroy`, sent as an explicit ORDERED broadcast with the package set to `com.envi.wispr.test`; the receiver sets the result to success only after `B.requestFocus()` returned true and `B.hasFocus()` reads true, and the row awaits that ordered result (`BroadcastReceiver` result on the sender's `onReceive`) before publishing READY; a refusal or a 10 s timeout is a staging failure; no manifest receiver, no manifest `android:exported`; coverage F6, grounded round 5), then publishes READY, the audio arrives, the audio-done broadcast arrives, stop; assert both receipts exist and BOTH read `""`, `insertionResult == copy_only`, `status == insertion_interrupted` (the take's final status on the fallback path; `awaitFinalRow` accepts `completed` and `insertion_interrupted` as final).
    86	T4. **`aDictationWithNoFieldToInsertIntoIsNotReportedToTheUserAsAFailure`** keeps its two phase waits, LISTENING before the audio and IDLE after `stopRequested` (already subject-fired, `VoicePipelineDeviceTest.kt:247-283`), and swaps the speaker playback for T2's audio argument (READY, then the audio-done broadcast, then STOP); `observedHandoff()` (a logcat read) stays as the PRECONDITION read (it classifies the staging, it does not judge the outcome), stated in its KDoc.
    87	T5. **Class declarations.** The rewritten rows are Product Outcome (when they fail, the user's words did not reach the field they were typing in, or reached it twice, or reached the wrong field); `transcribesThenPolishesWithSavedCustomWords` unchanged (Product Outcome, the polish limb).

   116	## 5. End-to-end state and lifecycle audit
   117	Async edges for the device rows (`code-design-rules.md` RULE: async-edge-case-enumeration): Interrupted (the driver dies before injecting: the take ends by the 3 s silence bound or the cap; the test's 90 s wait names the phase seen; `restore()` pays the journal) · Deleted (the receipt file removed mid-run: the read fails loudly, not as `""`, because the row asserts the file EXISTS then its text) · Mutated (a second take writes the receipt: `count(EXPECTED) == 1` fails) · Concurrent (two instrumentation runs: `am instrument` serialises per runner; the harness refuses when `pidof` shows one running) · Absent (no History row within the bound: the failure names the rows seen; fail closed) · Stale (an old row: the row awaited must be CREATED after the test's start timestamp, read from `createdAtMs`).
   118	For the harness: the `log_since` (proposed) key unset → `bounded: False` in the result and the verdict says so; a journal migration racing another session → under `_journal_locked`, first-writer moves, second finds nothing to move.
   119	
   120	### 5.1 Every wait in this change is a signal
   121	Rebuilt after grounded round 5 from `grep -n -i 'wait\|sleep\|poll\|settle\|bound\|timeout\|await' docs/feature-requests/issue-161-2026-09-22-harness-true-verdicts.md` over §3 and §4, one row per hit that names a wait; the two settles that precede a real signal are named so nobody mistakes them for one.

   125	| `_wait_for_owner_listening` (H5, T2 harness) | subject → harness | the owner's `Recording started (live after …)` line on one blocking `logcat -T` stream | 20 s → `BLOCKED` |
   126	| `_wait_for_the_take_to_finish` (H5) | subject → harness | the take's `insertion api=` or `Take ended: …` line on one blocking stream | 90 s → `UNKNOWN` |

   129	| `run_device_test` result (T2) | test → harness | the runner's final status code on the same stream | the runner's own |
   130	| T1/T3/T4 LISTENING | subject → test | `OnSharedPreferenceChangeListener` on the owner's phase | 20 s, names the phase seen |
   131	| T3 focus moved to B | rig → test | the ORDERED `ACTION_FOCUS_B` result, success only when `B.hasFocus()` | 10 s → staging failure |

   158	| `screenrecord` left running | n/a | n/a | killed by pid; only after the pid is gone is the file deleted and the debt settled; a pid that will not exit keeps the debt and is reported |
   159	| `ro.serialno` unreadable | `device()` refuses (identity unknown) | same | pays the transport-keyed book and says so |

   161	## 8. Caller-visible signals audit
   162	Verdict lines are the signals: `VERIFIED:` only on the exact editor text AND the parsed outcome; `ISSUE:` names the field that failed; `NOT RUN:` for recording off; `BLOCKED:` for a precondition the caller can fix; `NOTE:` for evidence (the route line, the recording path).
   163	
   164	## 9. Fallback source-of-truth audit
   165	The editor's own text (receipt file on the rig; the tree read on Gmail) is the truth for "landed"; the History row is the truth for the route; the log is diagnostic only. Where they disagree the row fails and prints all three.

   110	def _journal_read():
   111	    """The book, or a refusal. NEVER an empty book standing in for an unreadable one.
   112	
   113	    Swallowing a read error returned `{}`, which reads as "nothing is owed" — the one answer that makes
   114	    a caller stop looking, about a phone that may have several settings changed.
   115	    """
   116	    try:
   117	        raw = _JOURNAL.read_text()
   118	    except FileNotFoundError:
   119	        return {}
   120	    except OSError as why:
   121	        raise Blocked(f"the restore book at {_JOURNAL} cannot be read: {why}") from why
   122	    try:
   123	        book = json.loads(raw)
   124	    except ValueError as why:
   125	        raise Blocked(
   126	            f"the restore book at {_JOURNAL} is damaged and will not be overwritten: {why}. "
   127	            "Read it by hand and put back whatever it names."
   128	        ) from why
   129	    if not isinstance(book, dict) or not all(isinstance(v, list) for v in book.values()):
   130	        raise Blocked(f"the restore book at {_JOURNAL} is not in the expected shape")
   131	    return book

   133	
   134	@contextmanager
   135	def _journal_locked():
   136	    """Hold the book across a read-modify-write, so two processes cannot overwrite each other.
   137	
   138	    `os.replace` makes each WRITE atomic, and that is not the same thing: two processes can both read
   139	    the same book, each add its own debt, and the second write erase the first. One phone means this is
   140	    rare rather than impossible, and a lost debt is a setting left on the founder's phone with nothing
   141	    recording it.
   142	    """
   143	    import fcntl
   144	    # RE-ENTRANT WITHIN THIS PROCESS. `flock` is held per open file description, so a second `open` and
   145	    # `LOCK_EX` from the SAME process blocks on itself, for ever, holding the phone's take open while it
   146	    # waits. `open_recorder` holds this across a start and anything it calls in there could reach the
   147	    # book again, so the shape has to be safe rather than merely currently-unused.
   148	    if _STATE.get("holding_journal"):
   149	        yield
   150	        return
   151	    _JOURNAL.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   152	    with _JOURNAL.with_suffix(".lock").open("a") as handle:
   153	        fcntl.flock(handle, fcntl.LOCK_EX)
   154	        _STATE["holding_journal"] = True
   155	        try:
   156	            yield
   157	        finally:
   158	            _STATE["holding_journal"] = False
   159	            fcntl.flock(handle, fcntl.LOCK_UN)

   162	def _atomic_change(function):
   163	    """Hold the book's lock across an ordinary synchronous call.
   164	
   165	    **WHAT THE TEST BESIDE THIS PROVES, AND WHAT IT DOES NOT.** It recognises certain debt calls and
   166	    checks they sit inside an explicit `with _journal_locked()` or under an exact synchronous decorator
   167	    stack. It handles a literal `getattr` and treats lambda and generator-expression bodies as deferred.
   168	    It does NOT prove that every phone change is journaled, resolve an arbitrary alias, or prove that
   169	    deferred work keeps the lock.
   170	
   171	    An earlier version of this docstring said the class could not be reopened. It cannot say that, and
   172	    saying it stopped the next reader looking — which is how two real defects reached a commit message
   173	    as facts.
   174	    """
   175	    def wrapped(*args, **kwargs):
   176	        with _journal_locked():
   177	            return function(*args, **kwargs)
   178	    wrapped.__name__ = function.__name__
   179	    wrapped.__doc__ = function.__doc__
   180	    wrapped.__wrapped__ = function
   181	    wrapped._holds_the_book = True
   182	    return wrapped

   185	def _journal_write(book):
   186	    """Replace the book in one step, so an interrupted write cannot leave a half-file.
   187	
   188	    A `write_text` that dies mid-way leaves unreadable JSON, and the reader above then refuses
   189	    everything — which is safe, and still worse than not being able to happen.
   190	    """
   191	    _JOURNAL.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   192	    beside = _JOURNAL.with_suffix(".writing")
   193	    beside.write_text(json.dumps(book, indent=2))
   194	    os.replace(beside, _JOURNAL)

   197	def _identity_of(transport):
   198	    """The hardware identity behind a transport serial (`ro.serialno`), read once per transport.
   199	
   200	    A property read that FAILS is not an identity: it is refused, never guessed, because a debt filed
   201	    under a guessed key is a debt nobody will pay.

   210	                      "book cannot be found")

   212	    return identity

   215	def _scope_key(scope):
   216	    """The book's key for a scope: the literal `host`, or the hardware identity behind a transport.
   217	
   218	    A transport that is NOT attached keeps the name it was filed under: nothing can be paid to it until
   219	    it is attached, and the moment it is, `_migrate_locked` moves its debts under the identity.
   220	    """
   221	    if scope == "host":
   222	        return "host"
   223	    transport = scope or _STATE["serial"]
   224	    cached = _STATE["identities"].get(transport)
   225	    if cached:
   226	        return cached
   227	    if transport not in {serial for serial, _ in devices()}:
   228	        return transport
   229	    return _identity_of(transport)

   232	def _migrate_locked(book, transports):
   233	    """Move debts filed under a TRANSPORT serial to the identity key of that hardware. Under the lock.
   234	
   235	    Older versions keyed the book by cable (`100.94.206.47:5555` and the USB serial were two books for
   236	    one phone, #161 finding 11). Every debt is MOVED, never dropped: the transport-keyed entries come
   237	    first (they are older), then the identity-keyed ones, with the first value per setting kept, the
   238	    same rule `_owe_locked` applies. Returns whether the book changed.
   239	    """
   240	    changed = False
   241	    for transport in transports:
   242	        if transport == "host" or transport not in book:
   243	            continue
   244	        identity = _scope_key(transport)
   245	        if identity == transport:
   246	            continue
   247	        moved = [tuple(e) for e in book.pop(transport)]
   248	        kept = [tuple(e) for e in book.get(identity, [])]
   249	        merged = list(moved)
   250	        for entry in kept:
   251	            if not any(_debt_key(old) == _debt_key(entry) for old in merged):
   252	                merged.append(entry)
   253	        book[identity] = [list(e) for e in merged]
   254	        changed = True
   255	    return changed

   335	# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   336	# THE RECORDING HALF OF THIS HARNESS IS OFF.
   337	#
   338	# Six review rounds, and every one found a different sequence that could leave a recording running on the
   339	# founder's phone with nothing recording that it was. Each fix was correct and structural — the raw start
   340	# was deleted, the take became a context manager, the take went into the on-disk book before the start
   341	# intent under a lock held across it, each take got its own id, and no take could begin until the phone
   342	# had been restored. Round six found another anyway: `restore()` decided what to do outside the lock, so
   343	# a second session could erase the debt of a recording that had started while it waited.
   344	#
   345	# The consequence was declared in round six's own prompt, before its verdict was read, and this is it.
   346	# The alternative is a seventh clever fix, and the evidence of five rounds is that the next one has one
   347	# more sequence in it.
   348	#
   349	# The reading and settings half is untouched and is most of what this tool does: `look`, `find`, `tap`,
   350	# `open_tab`, `nav`, `scan`, `switches`, `set_switch`, `overlay`, `recording`, `logs`, `last_take`,
   351	# `restore`. Only the four calls that START a recording refuse.
   352	#
   353	# TO TURN IT BACK ON, delete this constant and the four guards that read it. Do that only on a review
   354	# round whose verdict says, in as many words, that the take-with-no-debt class is CLOSED — not that the
   355	# latest instance is fixed. Round six's `## CLASS` section is the shape of answer required.
   356	RECORDING_IS_OFF = (
   357	    "recording from this harness is off. Six review rounds each found a different sequence that could "
   358	    "leave a recording running on the phone with nothing recording it, so the recording half is not "
   359	    "offered until a round says the class is closed. Everything that READS the phone still works. "
   360	    "(The EMULATOR is exempt: a take leaked there costs nothing and restore() ends it.)"
   361	)

   377	def _run(args, timeout=60):
   378	    done = subprocess.run(args, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=timeout)
   379	    return done.returncode, done.stdout, done.stderr

   381	
   382	def _adb(command, timeout=60, check=True, serial=None):
   383	    """One `adb shell` command, with the REMOTE status read and, by default, enforced.
   384	
   385	    `serial` names a device OTHER than the selected one, for `restore()` putting back a debt owed to an
   386	    attached device this process is not driving. Nothing else passes it.
   387	
   388	    Two separate traps, and this function is where both are closed.
   389	
   390	    `adb shell` returns adb's OWN status, never the command's, so a check written without a sentinel
   391	    reports success whatever happened (`android-tooling.md` RULE: adb-shell-needs-a-sentinel). And a
   392	    sentinel that is read but ignored is the same bug one step later: `input tap` failing while `tap()`
   393	    returned "pressed ..." is a false pass about an action that did not happen.
   394	
   395	    `check=False` is for commands whose non-zero status is an ANSWER rather than a failure, such as a
   396	    `grep` that legitimately finds nothing. It is never a way to ignore an error.
   397	
   398	    The command is quoted into a single `sh -c` argument so that no caller-supplied text can be read as
   399	    shell syntax.
   400	    """
   401	    target = serial or device()
   402	    code, out, err = _run(
   403	        [ADB, "-s", target, "shell", f"sh -c {shlex.quote(command)}; echo __RC=$?"], timeout=timeout
   404	    )

   417	def devices():
   418	    """Every attached device, as (serial, model). Emulators included, and labelled as themselves."""
   419	    code, out, _ = _run([ADB, "devices", "-l"])
   420	    if code != 0:

   424	        if not line.strip() or "\tdevice" not in line and " device " not in line:
   425	            continue

   427	        model = re.search(r"model:(\S+)", line)

   441	    device has to be named, because "the attached device" is a question an emulator answers too, and a
   442	    stranger's phone answers it as readily as the founder's.

   453	        return _STATE["serial"]

  2294	def freeze_process(name=f"{PACKAGE}:audio"):
  2295	    """Freeze one of OUR processes on the EMULATOR through a debugger, by a pid verified in this call, journaled.
  2296	
  2297	    For the #115 scenes: a frozen capture process is the wedge the owner must end a take over. The debt
  2298	    (device pid, name, forwarded port, debugger pid) goes into the book BEFORE the debugger is spawned and is
  2299	    completed once its pid is known, so `restore()` from any later process thaws it by ending that debugger.
  2300	    Verified by the debugger's own line in its log, then by a binder probe that takes its timeout.
  2301	    """
  2302	    _require_emulator("freezing a process")
  2303	    pid = _the_one_process(name, "freezing")
  2304	    port = 18700 + pid % 1000
  2305	    log_path = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(_JOURNAL), f"jdb-{pid}.log")
  2306	    frozen = {"pid": pid, "name": name, "port": port, "host_pid": None}
  2307	    entry = ("frozen-process", json.dumps(frozen, sort_keys=True))
  2308	    with _journal_locked():
  2309	        _owe_locked(entry, device())
  2310	        if entry not in _owed():
  2311	            raise Blocked("the freeze could not be written to the restore book, so it was not done")
  2312	        _adb_host(["forward", f"tcp:{port}", f"jdwp:{pid}"])
  2313	        frozen["host_pid"] = _spawn_debugger(port, log_path)
  2314	        complete = ("frozen-process", json.dumps(frozen, sort_keys=True))
  2315	        _settled_locked(entry, device())
  2316	        _owe_locked(complete, device())
  2317	    for _ in range(100):
  2318	        time.sleep(0.1)
  2319	        try:
  2320	            with open(log_path) as f:
  2321	                if JDB_SUSPENDED in f.read():
  2322	                    break
  2323	        except FileNotFoundError:
  2324	            pass
  2325	    else:
  2326	        thaw_process(name)
  2327	        raise Blocked(f"the debugger never reported {JDB_SUSPENDED!r} for pid {pid} ({name}); see {log_path}. Thawed.")
  2328	    if _process_answers(pid):
  2329	        thaw_process(name)
  2330	        raise Blocked(f"pid {pid} ({name}) still answers a binder call after the suspend. Thawed.")
  2331	    return f"froze {name} pid {pid} (debugger pid {frozen['host_pid']} on port {port})"
  2332

  2334	def _adb_host(args):
  2335	    """An adb command that is not `shell` (forward, forward --remove) against the selected device."""
  2336	    done = subprocess.run([ADB, "-s", device(), *args], capture_output=True, text=True)
  2337	    if done.returncode != 0:
  2338	        raise Blocked(f"adb {' '.join(args)} failed: {(done.stderr or done.stdout).strip()}")
  2339	    return done.stdout.strip()

  2342	def _thaw(frozen):
  2343	    """End the debugger the book recorded, drop the forward, and read back that the process answers."""
  2344	    lines = []
  2345	    if frozen.get("host_pid") is not None:
  2346	        lines.append(_kill_debugger(frozen["host_pid"], frozen["port"]))
  2347	    if frozen.get("port") is not None:
  2348	        subprocess.run([ADB, "-s", device(), "forward", "--remove", f"tcp:{frozen['port']}"], capture_output=True, text=True)
  2349	    state = dict(_processes_named(frozen["name"])).get(frozen["pid"])
  2350	    if state is None:
  2351	        lines.append(f"{frozen['name']} pid {frozen['pid']} is gone; nothing to thaw")
  2352	        return "; ".join(lines)
  2353	    if not _process_answers(frozen["pid"]):
  2354	        raise Blocked(f"pid {frozen['pid']} ({frozen['name']}) still does not answer after the debugger ended")
  2355	    lines.append(f"thawed {frozen['name']} pid {frozen['pid']}")
  2356	    return "; ".join(lines)

  2359	@_atomic_change
  2360	def thaw_process(name=f"{PACKAGE}:audio"):
  2361	    """Thaw the process `freeze_process` froze, from the book, and settle the debt. Under the book's lock
  2362	    for the whole read-act-settle, like `restore()`."""
  2363	    _require_emulator("thawing a process")
  2364	    lines = []
  2365	    for entry in list(_owed()):
  2366	        if entry[0] != "frozen-process":
  2367	            continue
  2368	        frozen = json.loads(entry[1])
  2369	        if frozen["name"] != name:
  2370	            continue
  2371	        lines.append(_thaw(frozen))
  2372	        _settled(entry)
  2373	    return lines or [f"no frozen {name} is in the book"]

  2376	def kill_process(name=f"{PACKAGE}:audio"):
  2377	    """SIGKILL one of OUR processes on the EMULATOR, by a pid verified in this call, from the app's own uid.
  2378	
  2379	    `run-as <package> kill -9 <pid>` lands (measured on the AVD 2026-09-21; the shell uid cannot signal an
  2380	    app process, and `-STOP` from `run-as` does not take). Android restarts a bound service's process on
  2381	    the next bind, so nothing is owed afterwards. A frozen process dies too; its thaw debt is settled by
  2382	    the gone-pid branch of `restore()`.
  2383	    """
  2384	    _require_emulator("killing a process")
  2385	    pid = _the_one_process(name, "killing")
  2386	    package = name.split(":")[0]
  2387	    code, why = _adb(f"run-as {shlex.quote(package)} kill -9 {pid}", check=False)
  2388	    if code != 0:
  2389	        raise Blocked(f"pid {pid} ({name}) would not die: {why.strip()}")
  2390	    for _ in range(30):
  2391	        if pid not in dict(_processes_named(name)):
  2392	            return f"killed {name} pid {pid}"
  2393	        time.sleep(0.1)
  2394	    raise Blocked(f"pid {pid} ({name}) is still in the process table 3 s after SIGKILL")

  2397	def _pcm_from_sentence(sentence, path):
  2398	    """Say a sentence into a raw PCM file the emulator's microphone accepts: s16le, mono, 48 kHz."""
  2399	    for tool in ("say", "ffmpeg"):
  2400	        if not _has_tool(tool):
  2401	            raise Blocked(f"{tool} is not available, so no audio can be made for the emulator")
  2402	    aiff = path + ".aiff"
  2403	    _checked(["say", "-v", "Samantha", "-r", "160", "-o", aiff, "--", sentence], timeout=180)
  2404	    _checked(["ffmpeg", "-hide_banner", "-loglevel", "error", "-y", "-i", aiff,
  2405	              "-f", "s16le", "-ar", "48000", "-ac", "1", path], timeout=180)
  2406	    # HALF A SECOND OF SILENCE ON THE END. The stream ends when the last packet is sent, and the last
  2407	    # word was cut to "to Ma." on the first live take (2026-09-20, 1.8 s of audio, no padding); the
  2408	    # recogniser needs the tail to hear the word end.
  2409	    with open(path, "ab") as tail:
  2410	        tail.write(bytes(48000))
  2411	    return path
  2412	
  2413	
  2414	PCM_CHUNK_BYTES = 9600  # 100 ms of s16le mono at 48 kHz
  2415	
  2416	
  2417	def _audio_packets(pcm_path, packets_path):
  2418	    """Chunk raw PCM into the newline-delimited JSON AudioPackets `injectAudio` streams.
  2419	
  2420	    Every packet carries its `format` AND a `timestamp` (microseconds, ascending). A packet with no
  2421	    timestamp crashed the emulator on 2026-09-13 (device-testing.md RULE:
  2422	    feed-emulator-audio-over-grpc-injectaudio-not-blackhole). This is the ONLY packet builder.
  2423	    """
  2424	    import base64
  2425	    data = Path(pcm_path).read_bytes()
  2426	    if not data:
  2427	        raise Blocked(f"{pcm_path} holds no audio")
  2428	    count = 0
  2429	    t0 = int(time.time() * 1_000_000)  # epoch microseconds, ascending 100 ms per packet
  2430	    with open(packets_path, "w") as out:
  2431	        for count, offset in enumerate(range(0, len(data), PCM_CHUNK_BYTES), start=1):
  2432	            out.write(json.dumps({
  2433	                "format": {"samplingRate": 48000, "channels": "Mono", "format": "AUD_FMT_S16"},
  2434	                "timestamp": t0 + (count - 1) * 100_000,
  2435	                "audio": base64.b64encode(data[offset:offset + PCM_CHUNK_BYTES]).decode(),
  2436	            }) + "\n")
  2437	    return count, len(data) / (48000 * 2)
  2438	
  2439	
  2440	def inject_audio(pcm_path):
  2441	    """Stream a PCM file into the EMULATOR's microphone while a take is listening.
  2442	
  2443	    The injected stream IS the microphone only while the app holds it open, so this refuses unless
  2444	    `recording()` says a take is live; injected before the microphone opens, the stream is dropped and
  2445	    the take records silence, which reads exactly like a product that heard nothing.
  2446	    """
  2447	    _require_emulator("audio injection")
  2448	    _grpc("getStatus")
  2449	    if not Path(pcm_path).is_file():
  2450	        raise Blocked(f"{pcm_path} is not a file")
  2451	    if not recording():
  2452	        raise Blocked("no take is listening on the emulator, so injected audio would be dropped. "
  2453	                      "Inject inside `with open_recorder():`.")
  2454	    packets = pcm_path + ".packets.jsonl"
  2455	    count, seconds = _audio_packets(pcm_path, packets)
  2456	    _grpc("injectAudio", stdin_path=packets, timeout=int(seconds) + 60)
  2457	    return f"injected {count} packets, {seconds:.1f}s of audio"

  3104	def clear_log():
  3105	    """Clear the ring buffer so an absent line means absent rather than left over.
  3106	
  3107	    **IT CHANGES THE PHONE AND CANNOT BE PUT BACK, and that is stated rather than journaled.** The log
  3108	    it discards is gone; a debt promising to restore it would be a lie, and a lie in the restore book is
  3109	    worse than an honest gap. What is lost is diagnostic history, never the founder's own data.
  3110	
  3111	    **It no longer RESIZES the buffer, and that is a deletion rather than a fix.** The old version
  3112	    widened it to 16M and journaled "the buffer was N K" from the first line of `logcat -g` — which
  3113	    names one buffer out of several, and silently journaled nothing at all when that line did not match,
  3114	    while still doing the resize. A debt that cannot describe the change it undoes is worse than no
  3115	    resize: it leaves the founder's phone altered and the book saying otherwise. Every take this tool
  3116	    measures is seconds long, so the default buffer holds it.
  3117	    """
  3118	    _adb("logcat -c")
  3119	    # THE WINDOW OPENS HERE (#161 H5). The device's own clock, so `-T` compares like with like; every
  3120	    # read below is bounded to it, and a read with no window says so instead of pretending.
  3121	    _, stamp = _adb("date '+%m-%d %H:%M:%S.000'")
  3122	    _STATE["log_since"] = stamp.strip() or None
  3123	
  3124	
  3125	def _log_window_args():
  3126	    """The `-T` argument bounding a logcat read to this process's window, or nothing when none is open."""
  3127	    since = _STATE["log_since"]
  3128	    return f"-T {shlex.quote(since)} " if since else ""
  3129

  3131	def keep_awake(minutes=30):
  3132	    """Hold the screen on for a long walk, and journal the old timeout so `restore()` puts it back.
  3133	
  3134	    A walk of every page takes longer than the founder's screen timeout, and a phone that sleeps
  3135	    mid-walk produces a refusal about the lock screen rather than an answer about the page. This is a
  3136	    change to HIS daily driver, so it is journaled like any other
  3137	    (`session-behavior.md` RULE: revert-the-phone-after-a-session).
  3138	
  3139	    It does NOT touch the lock itself. A locked phone is refused, never opened: waking and swiping does
  3140	    not defeat a secure lock, and a harness that tries is doing something nobody asked it to.
  3141	    """
  3142	    if not isinstance(minutes, int) or isinstance(minutes, bool) or not 1 <= minutes <= 60:
  3143	        raise Blocked("minutes must be a whole number from 1 to 60")
  3144	    # UNDER THE LOCK, for the same reason `restore()` is: another session could settle this debt in the
  3145	    # gap between writing it and making the change, leaving the founder's screen timeout altered with
  3146	    # nothing recording it.
  3147	    with _journal_locked():
  3148	        _, previous = _adb("settings get system screen_off_timeout")
  3149	        previous = previous.strip()
  3150	        if not previous.isdigit():
  3151	            raise Blocked(
  3152	                f"could not read the phone's screen timeout, so it could not be safely changed: {previous!r}")
  3153	        # Owed BEFORE the write. A note about a change that did not happen is a wasted restore; a change
  3154	        # with no note is a setting left on the founder's phone.
  3155	        _owe_locked(("screen-timeout", previous), device())
  3156	        wanted = str(minutes * 60_000)
  3157	        _adb(f"settings put system screen_off_timeout {wanted}")
  3158	        _, actual = _adb("settings get system screen_off_timeout")
  3159	        if actual.strip() != wanted:
  3160	            raise Blocked("the screen timeout did not change, so the note about it would be wrong")
  3161	        return f"screen stays on for {minutes} minutes (was {int(previous) // 1000}s)"
  3162

  3164	def logs(pattern=None, lines=200):
  3165	    """The app's own log lines, newest last.
  3166	
  3167	    `lines` is validated rather than interpolated: it reaches a shell command, and a string there would
  3168	    carry whatever the caller put in it.
  3169	    """
  3170	    if not isinstance(lines, int) or isinstance(lines, bool) or not 1 <= lines <= 10000:
  3171	        raise Blocked("lines must be a whole number from 1 to 10000")
  3172	    # LOGCAT'S OWN TAG FILTER, not a pipe into grep. Two things were wrong with the pipe: a pipeline
  3173	    # reports its LAST command's status, so a failed `logcat` looked like a successful empty read, and
  3174	    # `check=False` then turned that into "nothing was logged" — which `recording()` reads as "the
  3175	    # microphone is closed". The filter also cannot be defeated by a tag containing a regex character.
  3176	    filters = " ".join(shlex.quote(f"{tag}:V") for tag in APP_TAGS)
  3177	    _, out = _adb(f"logcat -d {_log_window_args()}-v time {filters} {shlex.quote('*:S')}", timeout=90)
  3178	    selected = out.splitlines()[-lines:]
  3179	    if pattern:
  3180	        selected = [line for line in selected if re.search(pattern, line)]
  3181	    return "\n".join(selected).strip()

  3184	_INSERTION_FIELDS = ("api", "route", "written", "returned", "evidence", "outcome", "attempts", "ms",
  3185	                     "overrun", "target")

  3187	
  3188	def _insertion_line(text):
  3189	    """The app's `insertion api=... route=... outcome=... target=...` line as a dict, or None.
  3190	
  3191	    One producer writes it (`paste/InsertionOutcomeLine.kt`), so the fields are read by name; a line
  3192	    missing a field yields None for that field rather than a guess (#161 finding 2).
  3193	    """
  3194	    match = re.search(r"insertion api=\S+.*$", text, re.M)
  3195	    if not match:
  3196	        return None
  3197	    line = match.group(0)
  3198	    return {field: (m.group(1) if (m := re.search(rf"\b{field}=(\S+)", line)) else None)
  3199	            for field in _INSERTION_FIELDS}

  3202	def last_take():
  3203	    """What the most recent dictation did, as a small dictionary a person can read.
  3204	
  3205	    Every field comes from a line the app itself wrote. A field is None when the app never said it,
  3206	    which is different from zero and is left different. `bounded` says whether the read was limited to
  3207	    this process's log window; `unknown` names why the take cannot be read (two takes in the window),
  3208	    with every other field None, so a caller that does not look for it still cannot mistake two takes
  3209	    for one (#161 finding 9).
  3210	    """
  3211	    text = logs(lines=400)
  3212	    bounded = _STATE["log_since"] is not None
  3213	    starts = list(re.finditer(r"(?m)^.*\brecording_start\b.*$", text))
  3214	    if not starts:
  3215	        raise Blocked("the log holds no take at all since it was last cleared, so there is nothing to "
  3216	                      "report on. Call clear_log() before the take you mean to measure.")
  3217	    if bounded and len(starts) > 1:
  3218	        return {"unknown": f"{len(starts)} takes in the window; which one is meant is a guess",
  3219	                "bounded": True, "started": True, "ended_by": None, "bytes": None, "seconds": None,
  3220	                "warned_at_ms": None, "hit_the_cap": None, "transcribed_chars": None, "handoff": None,
  3221	                "insertion": None, "insertions": len(re.findall(r"insertion api=", text))}
  3222	    # ONE TAKE, THE LAST ONE. Every field below is the FIRST match in the text, so with two takes in
  3223	    # the log a run could report one take's ending beside another take's character count and read as a
  3224	    # single coherent result; an unbounded read keeps the old last-start rule.
  3225	    text = text[starts[-1].start():]
  3226	
  3227	    def first(pattern, cast=str):
  3228	        match = re.search(pattern, text)
  3229	        if not match:
  3230	            return None
  3231	        try:
  3232	            return cast(match.group(1))
  3233	        except (ValueError, IndexError):
  3234	            return None
  3235	    return {
  3236	        "started": "recording_start" in text,
  3237	        "ended_by": first(r"Stopped by ([^.]+)\."),
  3238	        "bytes": first(r"Stopped by [^.]+\. (\d+) bytes", int),
  3239	        "seconds": first(r"\((\d+\.\d+)s\)", float),
  3240	        "warned_at_ms": first(r"Duration warning shown at (\d+)ms", int),
  3241	        "hit_the_cap": "Take ended at the duration cap" in text,
  3242	        "transcribed_chars": first(r"Transcription result received \(chars=(\d+)\)", int),
  3243	        "handoff": first(r"handoff=(\w+)"),
  3244	        # The app writes `insertion api=... route=... outcome=...`; the older `Insertion completed via`
  3245	        # key matched a line no producer writes (`grep -rn 'Insertion completed via' app/src` is empty).
  3246	        "insertion": _insertion_line(text),
  3247	        "insertions": len(re.findall(r"insertion api=", text)),
  3248	        "bounded": bounded,
  3249	        "unknown": None,
  3250	    }

  3254	# Putting the phone back

  3257	def _checked(args, timeout=60):
  3258	    """Run a local command and refuse to continue when it failed."""
  3259	    code, out, err = _run(args, timeout=timeout)
  3260	    if code != 0:
  3261	        raise Blocked(f"{args[0]} failed: {err.strip() or f'status {code}'}")
  3262	    return out

  3265	@_atomic_change
  3266	@contextmanager
  3267	def _as_serial(serial):
  3268	    """Drive ANOTHER attached device for the length of a restore, and put the selection back.
  3269	
  3270	    Not `_switch_to`: that refuses while the selected device has debts, and a restore is exactly the
  3271	    moment it does. The selection is put back in `finally` so `device()` and `restored_for` are what
  3272	    they were, whichever device's debt was just settled.
  3273	    """
  3274	    if serial is None or serial == _STATE["serial"]:
  3275	        yield
  3276	        return
  3277	    saved = {k: _STATE[k] for k in ("serial", "tree", "eye", "eye_retry_after")}
  3278	    _STATE.update({"serial": serial, "tree": None, "eye": None, "eye_retry_after": 0})
  3279	    try:
  3280	        yield
  3281	    finally:
  3282	        _STATE.update(saved)

  3285	def _restore_one(entry, serial=None):
  3286	    """Put ONE change back, and read it back to prove it took.
  3287	
  3288	    `serial` names an attached device other than the selected one; see `_as_serial`.
  3289	
  3290	    A restore that returned zero is not a restore that happened.
  3291	
  3292	    **THE DEBT HAS TO BE IN THE BOOK.** Handed one that is not, this changed the founder's phone to a
  3293	    value nobody had recorded — `_restore_one(("screen-timeout", "1800000"))` against an empty book set
  3294	    his timeout to half an hour and left no trace. "Undo" is only undo when there is something to undo.
  3295	    """
  3296	    with _as_serial(serial):
  3297	        _restore_one_here(entry)

  3300	def _restore_one_here(entry):
  3301	    what, previous = entry

  3305	            f"there is no record of {what!r} having been changed on {scope}, so putting it 'back' to "

  3311	        if now != str(int(previous)):
  3312	            raise Blocked(f"the Mac's volume did not go back to {previous}; it reads {now}")
  3313	    elif what == "mac-input":
  3314	        _checked([SWITCH_AUDIO, "-t", "input", "-s", previous])
  3315	        now = _mac_input()
  3316	        if now != previous:
  3317	            raise Blocked(f"the Mac's input did not go back to {previous!r}; it reads {now!r}")

  3319	        # A debt written by the old `clear_log`, which named one buffer out of several. It cannot be
  3320	        # settled honestly, so it is not settled. `logcat -G <size>` by hand is the fix.
  3321	        raise Blocked(
  3322	            f"a log-buffer debt of {previous!r} was left by an older version that could not name which "

  3324	            f"entry from {_JOURNAL}."
  3325	        )

  3327	        # A RECORDING THAT OUTLIVED THE PROCESS THAT STARTED IT. Only a debt on disk can carry this, and
  3328	        # only a later session can act on it, so this is the whole point of the take being journaled.
  3329	        cancel_dictation()
  3330	        time.sleep(1.5)
  3331	        if recording():
  3332	            stop_app()
  3333	            time.sleep(1.5)

  3340	        # failed move over a different file already there settled the debt and left the real fixture
  3341	        # parked, changing what every later run plays.
  3342	        moved, why = _adb(f"run-as {TEST_PACKAGE} mv {shlex.quote(previous)} cache/{UAT_FIXTURE}",

  3345	            raise Blocked(f"the recorded fixture would not move back from {previous!r}: {why.strip()}")

   276	def _debt_key(entry):

   291	def _owe(entry, serial=None):

   298	def _owe_locked(entry, serial):

  3435	def _restore_locked():
  3436	    """Put back everything this session changed, and say what.
  3437	
  3438	    An entry is removed ONLY after its restore was read back. A failure leaves it in the journal and
  3439	    raises, so the next call tries again rather than reporting a rollback that did not happen.
  3440	
  3441	    **What this does NOT cover, said plainly rather than implied:** a dictation this tool started and
  3442	    the History row it produced, the app's foreground state, and onboarding once dismissed. This is the
  3443	    founder's daily driver: those are not restored, app data is never wiped and `com.envi.wispr` is
  3444	    never uninstalled from here, and no report should say otherwise.
  3445	    """
  3446	    _STATE["restored_for"] = None
  3447	    done = []
  3448	    # The HOST book first, then this device's, then any OTHER device in the book that is attached right
  3449	    # now. A debt owed to a device that is not attached cannot be verified, so it is reported and kept,
  3450	    # never settled: an unverified restore is not a restore. The other attached devices are driven
  3451	    # through `_as_serial`, so the selection this process made is untouched afterwards.
  3452	    selected = device()
  3453	    attached = [serial for serial, _ in devices()]
  3454	    # EVERY ATTACHED TRANSPORT IS RESOLVED TO ITS HARDWARE FIRST (#161 H7): debts filed under a cable's
  3455	    # name are moved under the identity they belong to, and each identity is paid through ONE attached
  3456	    # transport that reaches it. A key that names no attached hardware is reported and kept.
  3457	    book = _journal_read()
  3458	    if _migrate_locked(book, attached):
  3459	        _journal_write(book)
  3460	    transport_for = {}
  3461	    for transport in attached:
  3462	        transport_for.setdefault(_scope_key(transport), transport)
  3463	    selected_key = _scope_key(selected)
  3464	    others = sorted(key for key in _journal_read() if key not in ("host", selected_key))
  3465	    for key in ["host", selected_key] + others:
  3466	        if key not in ("host", selected_key) and key not in transport_for:
  3467	            if _journal_read().get(key):
  3468	                done.append(f"{key}: not attached, debt kept")
  3469	            continue
  3470	        scope = "host" if key == "host" else transport_for.get(key, selected)
  3471	        where = "" if key in ("host", selected_key) else f" on {scope} [{key}]"
  3472	        for entry in list(_owed(scope)):
  3473	            _restore_one(entry, serial=None if key in ("host", selected_key) else scope)
  3474	            _settled_locked(entry, scope)
  3475	            done.append(f"{entry[0]} back to {entry[1]}{where}")
  3476	    _STATE["restore"] = []
  3477	    # THE SCREEN DUMP IS NOT DELETED, and that is the whole of it. `rm -f` on a fixed path removes
  3478	    # whatever is sitting there, and "it looks like XML" is a description, not proof of ownership: a
  3479	    # file of the founder's with that name and that shape would go silently. It is one small file that
  3480	    # every run overwrites, so leaving it costs nothing and deleting it can cost something that is not
  3481	    # ours to spend.
  3482	    _STATE["restored_for"] = device()
  3483	    return done or ["nothing was changed"]

  2754	def run_device_test(test, sentence, expected_final=None, timeout=240, starve=False):
  2755	    """Run ONE instrumented row on the EMULATOR with this harness feeding its audio (#161 T2).
  2756	
  2757	    The row (`VoicePipelineDeviceTest`, `-e audio external`) starts the take itself, publishes
  2758	    `driver_phase=READY` on the instrumentation stream once the owner reported LISTENING and its staging
  2759	    is done, and waits for this driver's audio-done broadcast before it stops the take. Here: the test
  2760	    APK must already be installed (`adb install -r app/build/outputs/apk/androidTest/debug/app-debug-androidTest.apk`;
  2761	    never `connectedAndroidTest`, which uninstalls the app); the sentence is rendered to PCM; the stream
  2762	    is read group by group; on the READY group carrying this run's token the audio is injected and the
  2763	    done broadcast sent; every other group is a runner result and is reported as VERIFIED or ISSUE.
  2764	
  2765	    `expected_final` is passed through as `-e expected_final`; the row's own literal is used when None.
  2766	    `starve=True` is the NEGATIVE CONTROL: the driver answers READY with the done broadcast and feeds no
  2767	    audio at all, so a row that still passes is a row that proves nothing. On the phone this refuses:
  2768	    the phone's driver is `scripts/uat/silent-audio/run.py`.
  2769	    """
  2770	    device()
  2771	    if _recording_is_off():
  2772	        return [f"NOT RUN: {RECORDING_IS_OFF}"]
  2773	    _require_emulator("driving a device test's audio")
  2774	    if not re.fullmatch(r"[A-Za-z_][\w.]*(#\w+)?", test):
  2775	        return [f"BLOCKED: {test!r} is not a test class or class#method"]
  2776	    _, path = _adb(f"pm path {TEST_PACKAGE}", check=False)
  2777	    if "package:" not in path:
  2778	        return [f"BLOCKED: the test APK {TEST_PACKAGE} is not installed; install it with "
  2779	                "adb install -r app/build/outputs/apk/androidTest/debug/app-debug-androidTest.apk"]
  2780	    if not bound():
  2781	        return ["BLOCKED: auto-paste is switched off, so nothing can be inserted. "
  2782	                "enable_auto_paste() turns it back on."]
  2783	    token = uuid.uuid4().hex
  2784	    scratch = os.path.join(os.environ.get("TMPDIR", "/tmp"), "wispr-eyes")
  2785	    os.makedirs(scratch, exist_ok=True)
  2786	    pcm = _pcm_from_sentence(sentence, os.path.join(scratch, f"utt-{uuid.uuid4().hex}.pcm"))
  2787	    report = [f"NOTE: {sentence!r} was rendered to {pcm}"]
  2788	    args = [ADB, "-s", device(), "shell", "am", "instrument", "-w", "-r",
  2789	            "-e", "class", test, "-e", "audio", "external", "-e", "driver_token", token]
  2790	    if expected_final is not None:
  2791	        args += ["-e", "expected_final", expected_final]
  2792	    args.append(TEST_RUNNER)
  2793	    restore()
  2794	    ready()
  2795	    report.append(f"NOTE: {_rest_host_mic_off()}")
  2796	    clear_log()
  2797	    fed = False
  2798	    rebound_checked = False
  2799	    results = []
  2800	    process = subprocess.Popen(args, stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT, text=True)
  2801	    deadline = time.monotonic() + timeout
  2802	    try:
  2803	        for group in _instrumentation_groups(process.stdout):
  2804	            if time.monotonic() > deadline:
  2805	                report.append(f"ISSUE: the instrumentation ran past {timeout} s; UNKNOWN")
  2806	                break
  2807	            if group.get("code") == DRIVER_READY_STATUS and group.get("driver_phase") == "READY":
  2808	                if group.get("driver_token") != token:
  2809	                    report.append("NOTE: a READY from another run was ignored (token mismatch)")
  2810	                    continue
  2811	                if starve:
  2812	                    report.append("NOTE: STARVED on purpose: no audio fed (the negative control)")
  2813	                else:
  2814	                    report.append(f"NOTE: {inject_audio(pcm)}")
  2815	                _adb(f"am broadcast -a {EXTERNAL_AUDIO_DONE} --es driver_token {token} {PACKAGE}")
  2816	                report.append("NOTE: audio done broadcast sent")
  2817	                fed = True
  2818	                continue
  2819	            if "INSTRUMENTATION_CODE" in group:
  2820	                results.append(group)
  2821	                break
  2822	            if group.get("code") == 1 and group.get("test") and not rebound_checked:
  2823	                # THE TEST HAS STARTED, so the app's process is up again. Instrumentation restarts that
  2824	                # process and the accessibility service dies with it; on this AVD the system does not
  2825	                # rebind it on its own (seen 2026-09-22: handoff=SERVICE_NOT_RUNNING), so the driver does,
  2826	                # journaled, and the row waits on the service's own liveness fact before its take.
  2827	                rebound_checked = True
  2828	                if not bound():
  2829	                    report.append(f"NOTE: the accessibility service was unbound by the instrumentation "
  2830	                                  f"restart; rebound: {enable_auto_paste()}")
  2831	                continue
  2832	            if group.get("code") in (0, -1, -2, -3, -4) and group.get("test"):
  2833	                results.append(group)
  2834	    finally:
  2835	        try:
  2836	            process.wait(timeout=30)
  2837	        except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
  2838	            process.kill()
  2839	        for line in restore():
  2840	            report.append(f"NOTE: restored {line}")
  2841	    if not fed:
  2842	        report.append("ISSUE: the row never said READY, so no audio was fed (staging, not the product)")
  2843	    for group in results:
  2844	        name = group.get("test", "")
  2845	        cls = group.get("class", "").rsplit(".", 1)[-1]
  2846	        code = group.get("code")
  2847	        if code == 0:
  2848	            report.append(f"VERIFIED: {cls}.{name} passed on the device")
  2849	        elif code in (-1, -2):
  2850	            stack = group.get("stack", "").strip().splitlines()
  2851	            report.append(f"ISSUE: {cls}.{name} FAILED: {stack[0] if stack else 'no message'}")
  2852	        elif code in (-3, -4):
  2853	            report.append(f"NOTE: {cls}.{name} was skipped by its own precondition: "
  2854	                          f"{group.get('stack', '').strip().splitlines()[:1]}")
  2855	        elif "INSTRUMENTATION_CODE" in group:
  2856	            if group["INSTRUMENTATION_CODE"] != "-1":
  2857	                report.append(f"ISSUE: the instrumentation ended with code {group['INSTRUMENTATION_CODE']}: "
  2858	                              f"{group.get('shortMsg') or group.get('stream', '').strip()[-200:]}")
  2859	    if not any(g.get("test") for g in results):
  2860	        report.append("ISSUE: the runner reported no test at all; UNKNOWN")
  2861	    return report

  2864	def open_page(url, package="com.android.chrome"):
  2865	    """Open a URL in a browser and assert the browser is on screen. Nothing is sent to anyone."""
  2866	    ready()
  2867	    remote, out = _adb(f"am start -a android.intent.action.VIEW -d {shlex.quote(url)} {shlex.quote(package)}",
  2868	                       check=False)
  2869	    if remote != 0 or "Error" in out:
  2870	        detail = out.strip().splitlines()[-1] if out.strip() else "no message"
  2871	        raise Blocked(f"{url} would not open in {package}: {detail}")
  2872	    for _ in range(6):
  2873	        time.sleep(0.5)
  2874	        _STATE["tree"] = None
  2875	        if package in {n["package"] for n in tree()}:
  2876	            return f"{package} is showing {url}"
  2877	    raise Blocked(f"{package} did not come to the front for {url}")

  2880	def launch_emulator(avd=PLAY_AVD, restart=False):
  2881	    """Boot the AVD with its control service on, and wait until Android says it is up.

  3104	def clear_log():
  3105	    """Clear the ring buffer so an absent line means absent rather than left over.
  3106	
  3107	    **IT CHANGES THE PHONE AND CANNOT BE PUT BACK, and that is stated rather than journaled.** The log
  3108	    it discards is gone; a debt promising to restore it would be a lie, and a lie in the restore book is
  3109	    worse than an honest gap. What is lost is diagnostic history, never the founder's own data.
  3110	
  3235	    return {
  3236	        "started": "recording_start" in text,
  3237	        "ended_by": first(r"Stopped by ([^.]+)\."),
  3238	        "bytes": first(r"Stopped by [^.]+\. (\d+) bytes", int),
  3239	        "seconds": first(r"\((\d+\.\d+)s\)", float),
  3240	        "warned_at_ms": first(r"Duration warning shown at (\d+)ms", int),
  3241	        "hit_the_cap": "Take ended at the duration cap" in text,
  3242	        "transcribed_chars": first(r"Transcription result received \(chars=(\d+)\)", int),
  3243	        "handoff": first(r"handoff=(\w+)"),
  3244	        # The app writes `insertion api=... route=... outcome=...`; the older `Insertion completed via`
  3245	        # key matched a line no producer writes (`grep -rn 'Insertion completed via' app/src` is empty).
  3246	        "insertion": _insertion_line(text),
  3247	        "insertions": len(re.findall(r"insertion api=", text)),
  3248	        "bounded": bounded,
  3249	        "unknown": None,
  3250	    }

  3254	# Putting the phone back

  3257	def _checked(args, timeout=60):
  3258	    """Run a local command and refuse to continue when it failed."""
  3259	    code, out, err = _run(args, timeout=timeout)
  3260	    if code != 0:
  3261	        raise Blocked(f"{args[0]} failed: {err.strip() or f'status {code}'}")
  3262	    return out
  3263	
  3264	
  3265	@_atomic_change
  3266	@contextmanager
  3267	def _as_serial(serial):
  3268	    """Drive ANOTHER attached device for the length of a restore, and put the selection back.
  3269	
  3270	    Not `_switch_to`: that refuses while the selected device has debts, and a restore is exactly the
  3271	    moment it does. The selection is put back in `finally` so `device()` and `restored_for` are what
  3272	    they were, whichever device's debt was just settled.
  3273	    """
  3274	    if serial is None or serial == _STATE["serial"]:
  3275	        yield
  3276	        return
  3277	    saved = {k: _STATE[k] for k in ("serial", "tree", "eye", "eye_retry_after")}
  3278	    _STATE.update({"serial": serial, "tree": None, "eye": None, "eye_retry_after": 0})
  3279	    try:
  3280	        yield
  3281	    finally:
  3282	        _STATE.update(saved)

  3285	def _restore_one(entry, serial=None):

  3541	def _stream_log_until(markers, seconds, tags):
  3542	    """ONE blocking `logcat` reader over this process's window; the first line carrying a marker wins.
  3543	
  3544	    Not a loop over `logcat -d` snapshots (#161, grounded rounds 3 and 4): a snapshot loop is a poll
  3545	    with a budget, and a budget that runs out reads exactly like the subject never answering. The
  3546	    timeout here is a failure bound, reported as None, never a product verdict.
  3547	    """
  3548	    filters = [f"{tag}:I" for tag in tags] + ["*:S"]
  3549	    since = _STATE["log_since"]
  3550	    args = [ADB, "-s", device(), "logcat", "-v", "time"] + (["-T", since] if since else []) + filters
  3551	    process = subprocess.Popen(args, stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.DEVNULL, text=True)
  3552	    # A TIMER, NOT select(): a text pipe reads ahead into its own buffer, so select() on the descriptor
  3553	    # can say "nothing new" while a whole line already sits unread in Python (the first run of this
  3554	    # missed the owner's live line that way). Killing the reader from a timer makes the blocking line
  3555	    # iterator return, and the bound stays a bound.
  3556	    timer = threading.Timer(seconds, process.kill)
  3557	    timer.start()
  3558	    try:
  3559	        for line in process.stdout:
  3560	            for marker in markers:
  3561	                if marker in line:
  3562	                    return marker
  3563	        return None
  3564	    finally:
  3565	        timer.cancel()
  3566	        process.kill()
  3567	        process.wait(timeout=5)
  3568	
  3569	
  3570	def _wait_for_the_take_to_finish(seconds=90):
  3571	    """Block until the dictation reaches an ending, and say which one. None on the bound."""
  3572	    return _stream_log_until(_TERMINAL_LINES + ("insertion api=",), seconds,
  3573	                             ("DictationSession", "PasteService", "AsrService"))
  3574	
  3575	
  3576	def _wait_for_owner_listening(seconds=20):
  3577	    """Block until the session owner says the take is live, the signal that the microphone is ready.
  3578	
  3579	    The owner writes `Recording started (live after ...)` once the capture process has admitted and
  3580	    written its first audio block; the capture process's own `recording_start` comes earlier and is not
  3581	    readiness (#161, grounded round 2). A timeout is a refusal about staging, never a verdict.
  3582	    """
  3583	    marker = _stream_log_until(("Recording started (live after",), seconds, ("DictationSession",))
  3584	    if marker is None:
  3585	        raise Blocked(f"the owner never said the take went live within {seconds} s, so no audio was "
  3586	                      "fed; the microphone was never ready for it")
  3587	    return "the owner reported the take live"

  3590	# THE WHOLE APP, AS IT IS ON SCREEN. Walked by hand on 2026-09-06 and checked against the code that
  3591	# produces it: `AppShell.kt`'s `AppDestination` for the four tabs and `SettingsPage` for the eight
  3592	# drawer pages, so a page added to the app and not to this list is a gap somebody can find rather than
  3593	# an invisible one. `scan()` reports any page it meets that is not named here.
  3594	TABS = ("History", "Dictionary", "Transcription", "AI Polish")
  3595	PAGES = ("What's New", "Appearance", "Microphone", "Sounds", "Clipboard",
  3596	         "Permissions", "Privacy", "Storage", "Open Source Licenses")
  3597	
  3598	
  3599	def _tab_row(package=PACKAGE):
  3600	    """The four tab labels along the bottom, found by the ONLY thing that distinguishes them.
  3601	
  3602	    **Every tab name is on screen TWICE**: once as the heading of the page you are on, and once in the
  3603	    tab bar. `find('History')` correctly refuses that as ambiguous, so something has to say which is
  3604	    which, and it must not be a coordinate somebody typed.
  3605	
  3606	    The rule is structural: the tab bar is the one horizontal band that holds ALL FOUR names. A heading
  3607	    is alone in its band. So the band is found by asking the app where its own four labels agree,
  3608	    which survives a different phone, a different text size and a moved bar.
  3609	    """
  3610	    nodes = [n for n in tree(refresh=False) if n["package"] == package and _label(n) in TABS]
  3611	    for candidate in nodes:
  3612	        top, bottom = candidate["bounds"][1], candidate["bounds"][3]
  3613	        band = [n for n in nodes if n["bounds"][1] < bottom and n["bounds"][3] > top]
  3614	        if len({_label(n) for n in band}) == len(TABS):
  3615	            return {_label(n): n for n in band}
  3616	    raise Blocked(
  3617	        "the four tabs are not all on screen, so which label is the tab bar cannot be told from which "
  3618	        "is a page heading. What is there:\n" + look(only_ours=True)
  3619	    )
  3620	
  3621	
  3622	def open_tab(name):
  3623	    """Move to one of the four main tabs.
  3624	
  3625	    The tab you are ALREADY on reports itself as `selected` and NOT clickable, which is Compose saying
  3626	    there is nothing to press. That is an answer, not a failure, so this says so instead of refusing.
  3627	    """
  3628	    if name not in TABS:
  3629	        raise Blocked(f"{name!r} is not one of the four tabs: {', '.join(TABS)}")
  3630	    open_settings()
  3631	    _stable_tree()
  3632	    node = _tab_row()[name]
  3633	    if node["selected"] or _holder(node, lambda n: n["clickable"]) is None:
  3634	        return f"already on {name}"
  3635	    holder = _holder(node, lambda n: n["clickable"])
  3636	    x, y = holder["centre"][0], node["centre"][1]
  3637	    _STATE["tree"] = None
  3638	    _adb(f"input tap {x} {y}")
  3639	    # READ BACK that the tab took, polling up to the old fixed wait; a tap that landed mid-animation
  3640	    # is reported here rather than by the next call failing to find a switch.
  3641	    deadline = time.monotonic() + 1.2
  3642	    while True:
  3643	        time.sleep(0.15)
  3644	        if on_screen(name):
  3645	            return f"opened {name}"
  3646	        if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
  3647	            raise Blocked(f"{name!r} was pressed at ({x}, {y}) but the app is not showing it 1.2 s later")
  3648	
  3649	
  3650	def scan(toggle=False):
  3651	    """Walk EVERY screen in the app and read every switch on it. One call.
  3652	
  3653	    With `toggle=True` each switch is flipped, read back, flipped again and read back, so the report
  3654	    says whether the switch actually MOVES rather than only what it currently reads. Every switch ends
  3655	    where it started, and a switch that will not come back is raised rather than left inverted.
  3656	
  3657	    This is the Android twin of the macOS `scan()`, and the page list is the app's own two enums rather
  3658	    than a description of them.
  3659	    """
  3660	    ready()
  3661	    found = []
  3662	    for name in TABS:
  3663	        open_tab(name)
  3664	        here, whole = _walk_this_screen()
  3665	        found.append(("tab", name, here, whole))

  3026	def _screenrecord_processes():
  3027	    """Every `screenrecord` on the device as (pid, full command line), read from /proc, never by name alone."""
  3028	    _, out = _adb("pidof screenrecord", check=False)

  3030	    for pid in out.split():
  3031	        if not pid.isdigit():
  3032	            continue
  3033	        _, cmd = _adb(f"cat /proc/{pid}/cmdline | tr '\\0' ' '", check=False)
  3034	        found.append((pid, cmd.strip()))
  3035	    return found

  1607

  1615	def _newest_recording_start():
  1616	    """The newest `recording_start` line the capture wrote, or None when the tail holds none.
  1617	
  1618	    The IDENTITY of that line is the witness a rolling tail cannot fake: a new take writes a new line
  1619	    with its own timestamp, while a tail that rolled older lines out still ends at the same newest line.
  1620	    A count of lines across two snapshots can read equal or lower after a new start (round G2).
  1621	    """
  1622	    lines = [line for line in logs(lines=600).splitlines() if "recording_start" in line]
  1623	    return lines[-1] if lines else None

  1627	    """Send one launcher press during a live take and prove afterwards that it did not begin a take.

  1630	    delivery turns the toggle into a start. The proof is the newest
  1631	    `recording_start` line before the press against the newest one seen during `deadline_s` after it
  1632	    (`_newest_recording_start`): a different line is a take this press began, which is cancelled before
  1633	    raising; a tail with no start line at all after the press is "cannot tell", which also raises, never
  1634	    "no new take". One quiet observation is not proof while a take can still be STARTING, so the whole
  1635	    deadline is watched.

  1643	                      "told from it; not pressing")

  2720	def _instrumentation_groups(stream):
  2721	    """Yield each `INSTRUMENTATION_STATUS` bundle with its following `INSTRUMENTATION_STATUS_CODE`.
  2722	
  2723	    Raw `am instrument -r` prints every key of a bundle as `INSTRUMENTATION_STATUS: key=value` (a value
  2724	    may run over several lines, a stack trace does) and then ONE `INSTRUMENTATION_STATUS_CODE: n`; the
  2725	    final `INSTRUMENTATION_RESULT:`/`INSTRUMENTATION_CODE:` pair ends the run and is yielded as a group
  2726	    with `code` None and the key `INSTRUMENTATION_CODE`.
  2727	    """
  2728	    bundle = {}
  2729	    key = None
  2730	    for raw in stream:
  2731	        line = raw.rstrip("\n")
  2732	        if line.startswith("INSTRUMENTATION_STATUS: "):
  2733	            body = line[len("INSTRUMENTATION_STATUS: "):]
  2734	            key, _, value = body.partition("=")
  2735	            bundle[key] = value
  2736	        elif line.startswith("INSTRUMENTATION_STATUS_CODE: "):
  2737	            code = line[len("INSTRUMENTATION_STATUS_CODE: "):].strip()
  2738	            yield {"code": int(code) if code.lstrip("-").isdigit() else None, **bundle}
  2739	            bundle, key = {}, None
  2740	        elif line.startswith("INSTRUMENTATION_RESULT: "):
  2741	            body = line[len("INSTRUMENTATION_RESULT: "):]
  2742	            key, _, value = body.partition("=")
  2743	            bundle[key] = value
  2744	        elif line.startswith("INSTRUMENTATION_CODE: "):
  2745	            bundle["INSTRUMENTATION_CODE"] = line[len("INSTRUMENTATION_CODE: "):].strip()
  2746	            yield {"code": None, **bundle}
  2747	            return
  2748	        elif key is not None:
  2749	            bundle[key] = bundle[key] + "\n" + line
  2750	    if bundle:
  2751	        yield {"code": None, **bundle}
  2752

  3724	def room_is_quiet(seconds=6):
  3725	    """Record for a few seconds playing NOTHING, and report what the microphone heard.
  3726	
  3727	    **THE ONE PROBE THAT SEPARATES THREE FAILURES THAT LOOK IDENTICAL.** A content check can fail
  3728	    because the audio never played, because it played too quietly, or because somebody in the room was
  3729	    talking over it — and the third is the dangerous one, since it returns a fluent, well-punctuated
  3730	    transcript with healthy timing that reads exactly like the product returning a wrong answer.
  3731	
  3732	    Measured 2026-09-06: a take with nothing played came back with 79 characters, and a later run asking
  3733	    for a German fixture returned "Usually, yeah." — the founder, sitting beside his phone. Both would
  3734	    have been filed against the speech engine.
  3735	
  3736	    Words here mean the room is occupied, so STOP and say so rather than recording a content failure.
  3737	    Silence here means the room is fine and a failed content check is about the audio or the product.
  3738	
  3739	    Non-disruptive by construction: it plays nothing, so it is safe to run while somebody is talking,
  3740	    which is exactly when it is needed.
  3741	    """
  3742	    if _recording_is_off():
  3743	        return [f"NOT RUN: {RECORDING_IS_OFF}"]
  3744	    ready()
  3745	    restore()
  3746	    clear_log()
  3747	    # `verify=False`: the subject is what the microphone HEARS, so the recorder's appearance is not
  3748	    # what this run is about, and refusing on it would refuse the probe on a phone whose pill is fine.
  3749	    with open_recorder(verify=False):

The first concrete false-verdict risk is in the runner result path: skipped/assumption-failed rows are only NOTES, and the shared report printer does not treat `NOT RUN` as non-success. I’m continuing through the instrumentation/threading code before consolidating findings.
